He found he was in a small cell-like apartment with apparently no outlet save that through which he had come.

He was able to breathe without difficulty, for the perfect system of ventilation throughout the dungeons of the Secret House had been its architect's greatest triumph.

It seemed hours that he waited there, though in reality it was less than twenty minutes after his entrance that the door swung open again and he was called out.

===Chinese===
片刻之后，T. B. 史密斯带着三名侦探，查看了波尔塔沃刚刚离开的那间屋子。不见人影，也不见有人在此蹲过牢的丝毫痕迹。波尔塔沃在黑暗里立了仿佛没有尽头的一段时间。他发觉自己待在一间小囚室模样的屋子里，除了进来的那道口，显然再无出路。

他呼吸无碍——秘密之家各处地牢里这套完善的通风系统，正是那位设计师最得意的杰作。

他在那里等了像是几个钟头，其实进门还不到二十分钟，门就重新旋开，他被唤了出去。

===Original===
Farrington was in the room now, Farrington with his trusty lieutenant, and behind them the one-eyed Italian desperado whom Poltavo remembered seeing in the power house one day, when he had been allowed the privilege of inspection.

Some slight change had been made in the room since he was there last. Poltavo's nerves were in such a condition that he was sensitive to this variation. He saw now what the change was. The table had been drawn back leaving the chair where it was fixed.

Yes, it was a fixed chair, he remembered that and wondered why it had been screwed to the wood block floor. Dr. Fall and the engineer grasped him roughly and hurried him across the room, thrusting him into the chair.

"What are you going to do?" asked Poltavo, white as death.

"That you shall see."

Deftly they strapped him to the chair; his wrists and elbows were securely fastened to the arms, and his ankles to the legs of the massive piece of furniture.

===Chinese===
法林顿已在房里，带着他那位得力的副手；两人身后，还站着那个独眼的意大利亡命徒——波尔塔沃记得，有一天获准参观宅子时，在动力房里见过此人。

自他上次来后，屋里有了些细微的变动。波尔塔沃的神经已绷到一触即颤的地步，对这点变化也敏感起来。此刻他看清变在何处了：桌子被挪开了，椅子却留在原处，固定未动。

是的，那是把固定的椅子——他记得这个，还纳闷过它为什么用螺钉铆在木砖地上。福尔医生和那名机械师粗暴地抓住他，把他拖过屋子，按进椅子里。

“你们要干什么？”波尔塔沃问，面无人色。

“待会儿你就见了。”

他们麻利地用皮带把他绑进椅子；手腕和臂肘牢牢扣在扶手上，脚踝锁死在这件沉重家具的椅腿上。

===Original===
From where he sat Poltavo confronted Farrington, but the big man's mask-like face did not move, nor his eyes waver as he surveyed his treacherous prisoner. Then Fall knelt down and did something, and Poltavo heard the ripping and tearing of cloth.

They were slitting up each trouser leg, and he could not understand why.

"Is this a joke?" he asked with a desperate attempt at airiness.

No reply was made. Poltavo watched his captors curiously. What was the object of it all? The two men busy at the chair lifted a number of curious-looking objects from the floor; they clamped one on each wrist, and he felt the cold surface of some instrument pressing against each calf. Still he did not realize the danger, or the grim determination of these men whose secret he would have betrayed.

"Mr. Farrington," he appealed to the big man, "let us have an understanding. I have played my game and lost."

"You have indeed," said Farrington.

===Chinese===
波尔塔沃坐在那儿，正对着法林顿；可那大汉那张面具似的脸纹丝不动，打量他这个背主的囚徒时，眼光也不晃一下。随后福尔跪下去做了点什么，波尔塔沃听见布帛撕扯的声音。

他们在把两条裤腿各割开一条，他不明所以。

“这是开玩笑吧？”他强作轻松地问。

无人应声。波尔塔沃好奇地打量着他的捕手们。这一切到底为了什么？椅边忙活的两人从地上提起几件样子古怪的物件；他们各在他手腕上扣了一具，他又觉出某种冰冷的器具面贴上了两边小腿。他仍未意识到危险，也未意识到这几个人——他本来要出卖其秘密的人——那份冷峻的决心。

“法林顿先生，”他向那大汉求告，“咱们讲个明白。我这场牌打输了。”

“你确实是输了。”法林顿说。

===Original===
They were the first words he had spoken.

"Give me enough to get out of the country," Poltavo appealed, "just the money that I have in my pocket, and I promise you that I will never trouble you again."

"My friend," said Farrington, "I have trusted you too long. You forced yourself upon me when I did not desire you, you thwarted me at every turn, you betrayed me whenever it was possible to betray me, or whenever it was to your advantage to do so, and I am determined that you shall have no other chance of doing me an injury."

"What is this foolery?" asked Poltavo, in a mixture of blind fear and rage. They had unlocked the handcuffs and taken them off him, and now for the first time Poltavo noticed that the curious bronze clamps on his wrists were attached by thick green cords to a plug in the wall.

He shrieked aloud as he saw this, and the full horror of the situation flashed upon him.

"My God," he screamed, "you are not going to kill me?"

===Chinese===
这是他开口说的头一句话。

“给我一笔出国的盘缠，”波尔塔沃央告道，“就给我口袋里这点钱，我向你担保，再不来找你们的麻烦。”

“我的朋友，”法林顿说，“我信你信得太久了。我本不想要你，是你自己硬贴上来的；你处处掣我的肘；凡是能出卖我的时候，或是出卖我于你有利的时候，你回回出卖我。我已拿定主意：再不给你半点伤害我的机会。”

“这是搞什么名堂？”波尔塔沃问，盲目又怕又怒。他们已开锁摘了他的手铐；直到此刻，波尔塔沃才注意到，腕上那两具古怪的青铜夹具，正由粗粗的绿线连着墙上一个插座。

看清这一层，他失声尖叫，处境的全部恐怖电光般穿身而过。

“上帝啊，”他尖叫，“你们不是要杀我吧？”

===Original===
Farrington nodded slowly.

"We are going to kill you painlessly, Poltavo," he said. "It was your life or ours. We do not desire to cause you unnecessary suffering, but here is the end of the adventure for you, my friend."

"You are not going to electrocute me?" croaked the man in the chair, in a hoarse cracked voice. "Don't say that you are going to electrocute me, Farrington! It is diabolical, it is terrible. Give me a chance of life! Give me a pistol, give me a knife, but fight me fair. Treat me as you will; hand me to the police, anything but this; for God's sake, Farrington, don't do this!"

The doctor reached down and lifted a leather helmet from the floor and placed it gently over the doomed man's head.

"Don't do it, Farrington." Poltavo's muffled voice came painfully from behind the leather screen. "Don't! I swear I will not betray you."

===Chinese===
法林顿缓缓点头。

“我们杀你，会让你毫无痛苦，波尔塔沃，”他说，“不是你的命，就是我们的命。我们不愿让你白受罪，不过你的这场历险，到此为止了，我的朋友。”

“你们不是要电死我吧？”椅子里的人嘶声哑气地问。“别对我说要电死我，法林顿！这太狠毒了，太可怕了。给我一条生路！给我一杆枪，给我一把刀，同我公平地斗一场！随你们处置我；把我交给警察也行，什么都行，就是别这样；看在上帝份上，法林顿，别这样！”

医生俯身从地上提起一顶皮盔，轻轻扣在那注定要死的人头上。

“别动手，法林顿。”波尔塔沃发闷的声音从皮盔底下痛苦地透出来。“别！我起誓，我绝不出卖你们。”

===Original===
Farrington made a little signal and the doctor walked to the wall and placed his hand upon a black switch.

"I will not betray you," said the man in the chair in hollow tones. "Give me a chance. I will not tell them anything that you—"

He did not speak again, for the black switch had been pressed down and death came with merciful swiftness.

They stood watching the figure. A slight quivering of the hands and then Farrington nodded and the doctor turned the switch over again.

Rapidly they unfastened the straps, and the limp thing which was once human, with a brain to think and a capacity for life and love, slipped out of the chair in an inanimate heap upon the ground.

So passed Ernesto Poltavo, an adventurer and a villain, in the prime of his life.

===Chinese===
法林顿略一示意，医生走到墙边，把手搭上一只黑色闸刀。

“我不出卖你们，”椅中人声音空洞地说，“给我一个机会。他们休想从我嘴里掏出你们半个字——”

他没有再说下去，因为黑色的闸刀已经按下，死亡仁慈而迅捷地降临了。

他们站着注视那具身躯。双手微微一颤，法林顿点了点头，医生把闸刀扳了回去。

他们飞快解开皮带；那团瘫软的东西——它曾经是人，有过会思考的头脑、有能力去生活和爱——从椅子里滑落，成了一堆没有生气的肉，摊在地上。

埃内斯托·波尔塔沃，一个冒险家，一个恶棍，就这样在盛年殒命。

===Original===
Farrington looked down upon the body with sombre eyes and shrugged his shoulders.

He had opened his mouth to speak and Fall had walked to the switchboard and was about to put the deadly apparatus out of gear, when a sharp voice made them both turn.

"Hands up!" it said.

The stone door, through which Poltavo had passed to his doom from the corridor without, was wide open, and in the doorway stood T. B. and a little behind him Ela, and in T. B.'s hand was a pistol.

===Chinese===
法林顿阴沉着眼，俯视那具尸体，耸了耸肩。

他张口正要说话，福尔也已走到配电板前，正要把那致命的家什断电停当——一声尖利的喝叫，让两人同时转过身来。

“举起手来！”

那扇石门——波尔塔沃正是从外面走廊经它走向了死地——此刻大敞着；门口站着 T. B.，身后半步是埃拉；T. B. 手里，握着一支手枪。

## XV / XV

===Original===
In the rectory at Great Bradley, Lady Constance Dex arose from a sleepless night to confront her placid brother at the breakfast table. The Reverend Jeremiah Bangley, a stout and easy man, who spent as much of his time in London as in his rectory, was frankly nonplussed by the apparition. He was one of those men, common enough, who accept the most extraordinary happenings as being part of life's normal round. An earthquake in Little Bradley which swallowed up his church and the major portion of his congregation would not have interested him any more than the budding of the trees, or a sudden arrival of flower life in his big walled garden. Now, however, he was obviously astonished.

"What brings you to breakfast, Constance?" he asked. "I have not seen you at this table for many years."

"I could not sleep," she said, as she helped herself at the sideboard to a crisp morsel of bacon. "I think I will take my writing pad to Moor Cottage."

He pursed his lips, this easy going rector of Little Bradley.

"I have always thought," he said, "that Moor Cottage was not the most desirable gift the late Mr. Farrington could have made to you." He paused, to allow her a rejoinder, but as she made no reply, he went on: "It is isolated, standing on the edge of the moor, away from the ordinary track of people. I am always scared, my dear Constance, that one of these days you will have some wretched tramp, or a person of the criminal classes, causing you a great deal of distress and no little inconvenience."

There was much of truth in what he said. Moor Cottage, a pretty little one-storied dwelling, had been built by the owner of the Secret House at the same time that the house itself had been erected. It was intended, so the builder said, to serve the purpose of a summer house, and certainly it offered seclusion, for it was placed on the edge of the moor, approached by a byroad which was scarcely ever traversed, since Bradley mines had been worked out and abandoned.

===Chinese===
在大布拉德利的牧师住宅里，康斯坦丝·德克斯夫人熬过了一个不眠之夜，起身来到早餐桌前，面对她那位心平气和的哥哥。杰里迈亚·班利牧师（Reverend Jeremiah Bangley）是个肥胖随和的人，待在伦敦的时间和待在牧师住宅里的不相上下，此刻见了这位不速之客，当真愣住了。他是那种屡见不鲜的人物——再离奇的事到了他眼里，也不过是人生日常的一部分。哪怕小布拉德利发生一场地震，把他的教堂和大半个教民都吞了下去，在他心里激起的波澜，也不会比树木抽芽、大围墙花园里百花骤然盛开更多。可眼下，他分明是吃了一惊。

“什么风把你吹来吃早饭了，康斯坦丝？”他问，“这张桌子跟前，我有好多年没见着你了。”

“我睡不着。”她说着，在餐具柜前给自己夹了一片焦脆的培根。“我想带上写字板去荒原小屋（Moor Cottage）。”

这位小布拉德利随遇而安的牧师抿了抿嘴唇。

“我一向认为，”他说，“荒原小屋算不上已故法林顿先生送给你的一份可心礼物。”他顿了顿，好让她回一句嘴，可她一言不发，他便接着说：“那地方孤零零的，立在荒原边上，离人家常走的道儿远着呢。亲爱的康斯坦丝，我一直担心，不定哪天就会有什么下三滥的流浪汉，或是罪犯一流的人物，给你惹来大大的惊吓、说不尽的麻烦。”

他的话颇有几分道理。荒原小屋是一栋漂亮的小平房，是秘密之家的主人在建那座宅子的同一时期盖起来的。据建房人说，本意是拿它当个别墅消夏，它也确实清静：小屋坐落在荒原边上，只有一条小路可通，而自从布拉德利的矿脉采空废弃之后，那条路便几乎绝了人迹。

===Original===
Many years ago when the earth beneath the moor had been tunnelled left and right by the seekers after tin and lead, Moor Cottage might have stood in the centre of a hive of industry. The ramshackle remains of the miners' cottage were to be seen on the other side of the hill; the broken and deserted headgear of the pit, and the discoloured chimney of the old power house were still visible a quarter of a mile from the cottage.

It suited the owner of the Secret House, however, to have this little cottage erected, though it was nearly two miles from the Secret House, and he had spared neither expense nor trouble in preparing a handsome interior.

Lady Constance Dex had been the recipient of many gifts from Mr. Farrington and his friends. There had been a period when Farrington could not do enough for her, and had showered upon her every mark of his esteem, and Moor Cottage had perhaps been the most magnificent of these presents. Here she could find seclusion, and in the pretty oak-panelled rooms reconstruct those happy days which Great Bradley had at one time offered to her.

"It is a little lonely," she smiled at her brother.

She had a good-natured contempt for his opinion. He was a large, lethargic man, who had commonplace views on all subjects.

"But really you know, Jerry, I am quite a capable person, and Brown will be nearby, in case of necessity."

===Chinese===
许多年前，荒原底下的地脉还被寻锡觅铅的人东掏西挖的时候，荒原小屋所在的地方，简直可以说正处在一窝矿业的中心。山坡另一头还看得见矿工住房的破烂残迹；离小屋四分之一英里外，矿井那废弃坍塌的井架和旧动力房那座烟熏火燎的烟囱，至今依然可辨。

不过，秘密之家的主人觉得盖这么一座小屋正合他的心意，虽然它离秘密之家差不多有两英里远；为了把屋里装点得富丽堂皇，他也不惜工本、不辞辛劳。

康斯坦丝·德克斯夫人收过法林顿先生和他朋友们的许多馈赠。有一段时期，法林顿待她好得无以复加，把表示敬重的种种礼物一股脑儿堆到她身上，而荒原小屋或许便是这些馈赠中最贵重的一件。在这里她可以觅得清静，在几间镶着橡木护壁板的漂亮屋子里，重温大布拉德利一度赠予过她的那些好时光。

“是有点冷清。”她对哥哥一笑。

她对哥哥的见解怀着一种好脾气的轻蔑。他是个大块头、懒洋洋的人，对什么事都是一副平庸见识。

“不过说真的，杰里（Jerry），我这人挺能照顾自己的，再说布朗就在近旁，真有事也使得上劲。”

===Original===
He nodded, and addressed himself again to the *Times*, the perusal of which she had interrupted.

"I have nothing more to say," he said from behind his newspaper. By and by he put it down.

"Who is this Mr. Smith?" he asked, suddenly.

"Mr. Smith?" she said, with interest. "Which Mr. Smith are you referring to?"

===Chinese===
他点点头，重新埋头读起被她打断的《泰晤士报》来。

“我没什么可说的了。”他从报纸后面说。过了一会儿，他把报纸放了下来。

“这位史密斯先生是什么人？”他突然问。

“史密斯先生？”她来了兴致，“你说的是哪一位史密斯先生？”

===Original===
"I think he is a detective person," said the Reverend Jeremiah Bangley; "he has honoured us with a great number of visits lately."

"You mean—?"

"I mean Great Bradley," he explained. "Do you think there is anything wrong at the Secret House?"

"What could there be wrong," she asked, "that has not been wrong for the last ten or twenty years?"

He shrugged his massive shoulders.

"I have never quite approved of the Secret House," he said, unnecessarily.

===Chinese===
“我看他是个侦探之类的人物。”杰里迈亚·班利牧师说，“近来他光临我们这地方，次数可不少。”

“你是说——？”

“我是说大布拉德利，”他解释道，“你觉得秘密之家是不是出了什么岔子？”

“还能出什么岔子，”她问，“是这十二十年里没出过的？”

他耸了耸宽厚的肩膀。

“我从来就不大看得惯秘密之家。”他说，这话纯属多余。

===Original===
She finished her hurried breakfast and rose.

"You have never approved of anything, Jerry," she said, tapping him on the shoulder as she passed.

She looked through the window; the victoria she had ordered was waiting at the door, with the imperturbable Brown sitting on the box.

"I shall be back to lunch," she said.

Looking through a window he saw her mount into the carriage carrying a portfolio. In that letter case, although he did not know it, were the letters and diaries which Dr. Goldworthy had brought from the Congo. In the seclusion of Moor Cottage she found the atmosphere to understand the words, written now in fire upon her very soul, and to plan her future.

===Chinese===
她匆匆吃罢早饭，站起身来。

“你就没看得惯过什么，杰里。”她走过时轻拍着他的肩膀说。

她往窗外望去；她吩咐备好的那辆维多利亚式马车正候在门前，一脸波澜不惊的布朗坐在车夫座上。

“我回来吃午饭。”她说。

他从一扇窗子里望见她带着一只文件夹登上了马车。他并不知道，那只文件夹里装的是戈德沃西医生从刚果带回的那些信件和日记。在荒原小屋的清静里，她寻得了领会那些字句的心境——那些字如今已如火烙一般印在她的灵魂上——也好筹划自己的将来。

===Original===
There was no servant at Moor Cottage. She was in the habit of sending one of her own domestic staff after her visit to make it tidy for her future reception.

She let herself in through the little door placed under the green-covered porch.

"You can unharness the horse; I shall be here two hours," she said to the waiting Brown.

The man touched his hat. He was used to these excursions and was possessed of the patience of his class. He backed the victoria on to the moor by the side of the fence which surrounded the house. There was a little stable at the back, but it was never used. He unharnessed the horse, fixed his nosebag, and sat down to read his favourite newspaper; a little journal which dealt familiarly with the erratic conduct of the upper classes. He was not a quick reader, and there was sufficient in the gossipy journal to occupy his attention for three or four hours. At the end of an hour he thought he heard his lady's voice calling him, and jumping up, he walked to the door of the cottage.

===Chinese===
荒原小屋里没有仆人。她习惯于每次来过之后，再从自家的仆妇里派一个来收拾齐整，好待下次迎候她。

她推开绿藤掩映的门廊下那扇小门，自己走了进去。

“把马卸了吧；我要待两个钟头。”她对候着的布朗说。

这人碰了碰帽子。这类出游他早已司空见惯，也生就了他那个阶层的耐心。他把马车倒到围着小屋的栅栏旁的荒原上。屋后有个小马厩，但从来不用。他卸下马，套上饲料袋，便坐下来读他心爱的报纸——一份专爱对上流阶层的越轨行径评头论足的小报。他读书不快，那份满是闲话的小报，足够他消磨三四个钟头。约莫过了一个钟头，他仿佛听见女主人在唤他，便一跃而起，朝小屋的门走去。

===Original===
He listened, but there was no other sound, and he came back to his previous position, and continued his study of the decadent aristocracy. Four hours he waited, and assailed by a most human hunger, his patience was pardonably exhausted.

He rose slowly, harnessed the horse, and drove the victoria ostentatiously before the window of the little sitting-room which Lady Constance Dex used as a study. Another half an hour passed without any response, and he got down from his box and knocked at the door.

There was no answer; he knocked again; still no reply.

In alarm he went to the window and peered in. The floor was strewn with papers scattered in confusion. A chair had been overturned. More to the point, he saw an overturned inkpot, which was eloquent to his ordered mind of an unusual happening.

===Chinese===
他侧耳听了听，再没有别的动静，便回到原处，继续钻研上流社会的颓靡。他等了四个钟头，一阵人之常情的饥饿袭来，他的耐心耗尽了——这也情有可原。

他慢慢起身，套好马，故意把车赶到康斯坦丝·德克斯夫人用作书房的那间小客厅的窗前。又过了半个钟头还是毫无回音，他便从车夫座上下来，去敲门。

无人应门；他又敲；仍是没有回音。

他慌了神，走到窗前往里窥看。地板上散乱地扔满了纸张。一把椅子翻倒在地。更要紧的是，他看见一只打翻的墨水瓶——在他那颗讲究条理的心里，这是出了非常变故的铁证。

===Original===
Increasingly alarmed, he put his shoulder to the door, but it did not yield. He tried the window; it was locked.

It was at that moment that a motor came swiftly over the hill from the direction of the rectory. With a jar it came to a sudden stop before the house, and T. B. Smith leapt out.

Brown had seen the detective before on his visits to the rectory, and now hailed him as veritably god-sent.

"Where is Lady Constance?" asked T. B., quickly.

The man pointed to the house with trembling finger.

"She's in there somewhere," he said, fretfully, "but I can't make her answer … and the room appears to be very disordered."

===Chinese===
他越发惊慌，用肩膀去撞门，门纹丝不动。他再去推窗；窗也锁着。

正在这时，一辆汽车从牧师住宅那个方向翻过山头疾驰而来，猛地一顿，停在小屋前面，T. B. 史密斯跳下了车。

布朗从前在牧师住宅见过这位侦探几回，此刻一见，真把他当成天降救星了。

“康斯坦丝夫人在哪儿？”T. B. 急忙问。

车夫用发抖的手指了指屋子。

“她就在里面什么地方，”他焦躁地说，“可我怎么叫她也不应……屋里像是乱得很。”

===Original===
He led the way to the window. T. B. looked in and saw that which confirmed his worst fears.

"Stand back," he said.

He raised his ebony stick and sent it smashing through the glass. In a second his hand was inside unlocking the latch of the window; a few seconds later he was in the room itself. He passed swiftly from room to room, but there was no sign of Lady Constance. On the floor of the study was a piece of lace collar, evidently wrenched from her gown.

"Hullo!" said Ela, who had followed him. He pointed to the table. On a sheet of paper was the print of a bloody palm.

"Farrington," said T. B., briefly, "he has been here; but how did he get out?"

===Chinese===
他领着路走到窗前。T. B. 往里一看，看见的情景证实了他最坏的担忧。

“让开。”他说。

他举起乌木手杖，一杖击碎了玻璃。转瞬间他的手已探进窗内，拨开了窗闩；几秒钟后，他已置身屋内。他飞快地逐屋搜去，却不见康斯坦丝夫人的踪影。书房的地板上有一截蕾丝衣领，显然是从她衣裙上撕扯下来的。

“喂！”跟在他身后的埃拉开了口。他指着桌子。一页纸上，印着一只血淋淋的手掌。

“法林顿。”T. B. 简短地说，“他来过这儿；可他是怎么出去的？”

===Original===
He questioned the coachman closely, but the man was emphatic.

"No, sir," he said, "it would have been impossible for anybody to have passed out of here without my seeing them. Not only could I see the cottage from where I sat, but the whole of the hillside."

"Is there any other place where she could be?"

"There is the outhouse," said Brown, after a moment's thought; "we used to put up the victoria there, but we never use it nowadays in fine weather."

The outhouse consisted of a large coachhouse and a small stable. There was no lock to the doors, T. B. noticed, and he pulled them open wide. There was a heap of straw in one corner, kept evidently as a provision against the need of the visiting coachman. T. B. stepped into the outhouse, then suddenly with a cry he leant down, and caught a figure by the collar and swung him to his feet.

"Will you kindly explain what you are doing here?" he asked, and then gave a gasp of astonishment, for the sleepy-eyed prisoner in his hands was Frank Doughton.

===Chinese===
他细细盘问车夫，车夫却一口咬定。

“不可能，先生，”他说，“谁要从这儿出去，绝逃不过我的眼睛。不光是小屋，从我坐的地方，整面山坡都尽收眼底。”

“她还可能在别的什么地方吗？”

“还有那间外屋，”布朗想了一下说，“从前我们常把马车停在那儿，如今天气好就再不用了。”

那间外屋包括一座大车房和一间小马厩。T. B. 留意到门上没有锁，便把它们统统推开。一角堆着一垛稻草，显然是备着来访车夫不时之需的。T. B. 迈步走进外屋，忽然喊了一声，俯身揪住一个人的领子，把他一把拽了起来。

“劳驾解释一下，你在这儿干什么？”他问，随即倒抽一口冷气——他手里那个睡眼惺忪的俘虏，竟是弗兰克·道顿。

---

===Original===
"It is a curious story you tell me," said T. B.

"I admit it is curious," said Frank, with a smile, "and I am so sleepy that I do not know how much I have told you, and how much I have imagined."

"You told me," recapitulated T. B., "that you were kidnapped last night in London, that you were carried through London and into the country in an unknown direction, and that you made your escape from the motorcar by springing out in the early hours of this morning, whilst the car was going at a slackened speed."

"That is it," said the other. "I have not the slightest idea where I am; perhaps you can tell me?"

"You are near Great Bradley," said T. B., with a smile. "I wonder you do not recognize your home; for home it is, as I understand."

===Chinese===
“你讲的这段事可真离奇。”T. B. 说。

“我承认是离奇，”弗兰克笑着说，“而且我困得不行，自己都分不清哪些是对你讲过的，哪些是我想象出来的。”

“你告诉我，”T. B. 复述道，“你昨夜在伦敦遭了绑架，被人载着穿过伦敦、往乡间一个不明的方向去；今天凌晨，趁车子放慢速度的当口，你纵身跳出车来，脱了身。”

“正是如此，”对方说，“我压根儿不知道自己在哪儿；也许你说得上来？”

“你这是在大布拉德利附近。”T. B. 微笑着说，“奇怪的是你竟认不出自己的老家；据我所知，这儿就是你的家呀。”

===Original===
Frank looked round with astonished eyes.

"What were they bringing me here for?" he demanded.

"That remains to be discovered," replied T. B.; "my own impression is that you—"

"Do you think I was being taken to the Secret House?" interrupted the young man, suddenly.

T. B. shook his head.

"I should think that was unlikely. I suspect our friend Poltavo of having carried out this little coup entirely on his own. I further suspect his having brought the car in this direction with no other object than to throw suspicion upon our worthy friends across the hill—and how did you come to the outhouse?"

"I was dead beat," explained Frank. "I had a sudden spasm of strength which enabled me to outdistance those people who were pursuing me, but after I had shaken them off I felt that I could drop. I came upon this cottage, which seemed the only habitation in view, and after endeavouring to waken the occupants I did the next best thing, I made my way into the coachhouse and fell asleep."

===Chinese===
弗兰克环顾四周，眼里满是诧异。

“他们把我弄到这儿来干什么？”他质问。

“这还有待查明，”T. B. 答道，“我个人的看法是，你——”

“你觉得他们是要把我送到秘密之家去？”年轻人突然插话。

T. B. 摇了摇头。

“我看未必。我疑心这出小小的突袭，全是咱们那位波尔塔沃朋友自个儿一手操办的。我进一步怀疑，他把车往这个方向开，无非是想把嫌疑栽到山那边那几位可敬的朋友头上去——对了，你是怎么跑到外屋去的？”

“我当时累散了架，”弗兰克解释道，“一时爆出一股子蛮劲，才把追我的人甩在了后头；可甩脱之后，我只觉得自己随时会瘫倒。我撞见这座小屋，放眼望去它是唯一的人家，我想叫醒屋里的人，没能成，便退而求其次，摸进车房，倒头睡着了。”

===Original===
T. B. had no misgivings so far as this story was concerned; he accepted it as adding only another obstacle to the difficulties of his already difficult task.

"You heard no sound whilst you lay there?"

"None whatever," said the young man.

"No sound of a struggle, I mean," said T. B., and then it was that he explained to Frank Doughton the extraordinary disappearance of the owner of Moor Cottage.

"She must be in the house," said Frank.

They went back and resumed their search. Upstairs was a bedroom, and adjoining a bathroom. On the ground floor were two rooms: the study he had quitted and a smaller room beautifully decorated and containing a piano. But the search was fruitless; Lady Constance Dex had disappeared as though the earth had opened and swallowed her up. There was no sign of a trap in the whole of the little building, and T. B. was baffled.

===Chinese===
对这番话，T. B. 没起半点疑心；他只当它给自己本已棘手的难题，又添了一重障碍。

“你躺在那儿的时候，没听见什么动静？”

“什么也没听见。”年轻人说。

“我是说，没有搏斗的声响。”T. B. 说。接着，他便向弗兰克·道顿讲了荒原小屋主人离奇失踪的经过。

“她一定还在屋里。”弗兰克说。

他们回到屋里，重新搜了一遍。楼上是间卧室，旁边带一间浴室。楼下有两间屋：他刚才待过的书房，和一间陈设精美、摆着一架钢琴的小屋。但这番搜寻一无所获；康斯坦丝·德克斯夫人消失得无影无踪，仿佛大地裂开，把她吞了下去。整座小楼里找不出半点暗门机关的迹象，T. B. 一筹莫展。

===Original===
"It is a scientific axiom," he said, addressing Ela with a thoughtful glint in his eye, "that matter must occupy space, therefore Lady Constance Dex must be in existence, she cannot have evaporated into thin air, and I am not going to leave this place until I find her."

Ela was thinking deeply, and frowning at the untidiness of the table.

"Do you remember that locket which you found on one of the dead men in Brakely Square?" he asked suddenly.

T. B. nodded. He put his hand in his waistcoat pocket, for he had carried that locket ever since the night of its discovery.

"Let us have a look at the inscription again," said Ela.

They drew up chairs to the table and examined the little circular label which they had found in the battered interior.

===Chinese===
“科学上有一条公理，”他眼里闪着深思的光，对埃拉说，“物质必定占据空间；所以康斯坦丝·德克斯夫人必定还在，她不可能蒸发到空气里去。不找到她，我不离开这个地方。”

埃拉正想得入神，对着那张凌乱的桌子皱眉。

“你还记得你在布雷克利广场一个死人身上找到的那只小金盒吗？”他突然问。

T. B. 点点头。他把手伸进马甲口袋——自发现它的那晚起，那只小金盒就一直带在他身上。

“咱们再看看那行刻字。”埃拉说。

他们把椅子拉到桌边，仔细端详从那砸坏了的盒芯里找到的小圆牌。

===Original===
> "Mor: Cot. 

 God sav the Keng."

Ela shook his head helplessly.

"I am perfectly sure there is a solution here," he said. "Do you see those words on the top? 'Mor: Cot.'—that stands for Moor Cottage."

"By Jove, so it does," said T. B., picking up the locket; "that never struck me before. It was the secret of Moor Cottage which this man discovered, and with which he was trying to blackmail our friend. So far as the patriotic postscript is concerned that is beyond my understanding."

"There is a meaning to it," said Ela, "and it is not a cryptogram either. You see how he has forgotten to put the *e* in 'save'? And he has spelt 'king' 'keng.'"

===Chinese===
> “Mor: Cot.（荒原小屋）

 上帝保佑国壬。”

埃拉束手无策地摇了摇头。

“我敢肯定这里头有个答案，”他说，“你看见顶上那几个字没有？‘Mor: Cot.’——那代表荒原小屋（Moor Cottage）。”

“天哪，可不是嘛，”T. B. 拿起小金盒说，“我从前竟没想到这一层。这人发现的正是荒原小屋的秘密，还想拿它敲咱们那位朋友的竹杠。至于后面那句爱国的附言嘛，我是全然参不透了。”

“那句话有名堂，”埃拉说，“而且还不是什么密码。你瞧，他把 save 里的 e 给落了；又把 king 拼成了 keng。”

===Original===
They waited before the house whilst Brown drove to the rectory, and then on to the town. Jeremiah Bangley arrived in a state of calm anticipation. That his sister had disappeared did not seem to strike him as a matter for surprise, though he permitted himself to say that it was a very remarkable occurrence.

"I have always warned Constance not to be here alone, and I should never have forgiven myself if Brown had not been on the spot," he said.

"Can you offer any explanation?"

The rector shook his head. He was totally ignorant of the arrangements of the house, had never, so he said, put foot in it in his life. This was perfectly true, for he was an incurious man who did not greatly bother himself about the affairs of other people. The local police arrived in half an hour, headed by the chief inspector, who happened to be in the station when the report was brought in.

"I suppose I had better take this young man to the station?" he said, indicating Frank.

===Chinese===
他们在屋前等候，布朗驱车先去牧师住宅，再到镇上。杰里迈亚·班利来了，一副心平气和、静候下文的样子。妹妹失踪了这件事，似乎并没有让他觉得意外，尽管他也表示，这实在是件非同小可的事。

“我一直告诫康斯坦丝别独自待在这儿；亏得布朗就在跟前，不然我永远不会原谅自己。”他说。

“你能给出什么解释吗？”

牧师摇了摇头。他对这屋子的格局一无所知，据他自己说，这辈子从没踏进一步。这倒千真万确，因为他是个不好奇的人，不大为别人的事操心。半小时后，本地警察赶到了，领头的是位探长——报案送到警局时，他恰好在局里。

“我看我最好把这个年轻人带回局里去？”他指着弗兰克说。

===Original===
"Why?" asked T. B. calmly; "what do you gain by arresting him? As a matter of fact there is no evidence whatever which would implicate Mr. Doughton, and I am quite prepared to give you my own guarantee to produce him whenever you may require him.
"The best thing you can do is to get back to town," he said kindly to that young man; "you need a little sleep. It is not a pleasant prelude to your marriage. By the way, that is tomorrow, is it not?" he asked, suddenly.

Frank nodded.

"I wonder if that has anything to do with your kidnapping," said T. B. thoughtfully. "Is there any person who is anxious that this marriage should not come about?"

Frank hesitated.

===Chinese===
“为什么？”T. B. 平静地问，“逮捕他对你有什么好处？事实上，没有任何一点证据能牵连到道顿先生；我也完全愿意以我个人的担保，你们什么时候要传他，我随时交人。
“你现在最好是回城去，”他和颜悦色地对年轻人说，“你需要睡一觉。这可不是你婚礼前该有的一段序曲。对了，婚期就是明天，是吧？”他突然问。

弗兰克点点头。

“不知你遭绑架的事，同这桩婚事有没有干系。”T. B. 若有所思地说，“有没有什么人，巴望着这门亲事结不成？”

弗兰克迟疑了。

===Original===
"I hardly like to accuse a man," he said, "but Poltavo—"

"Poltavo?" repeated T. B. quickly.

"Yes," said Frank; "he has some views on the question of Miss Gray."

He spoke reluctantly, for he was loath to introduce Doris' name into the argument.

"Poltavo would have a good reason," mused T. B. Smith. "Tell me what happened in the car."

Briefly Frank related the circumstances which had led up to his capture.

===Chinese===
“我实在不愿无端指认什么人，”他说，“可波尔塔沃——”

“波尔塔沃？”T. B. 立刻追问。

“是啊，”弗兰克说，“在格雷小姐的问题上，他自有他的盘算。”

他说得很勉强，因为他不愿把多丽丝的名字牵进这场谈话。

“波尔塔沃倒是有个现成的动机。”T. B. 史密斯沉吟道，“把车里发生的事给我讲讲。”

弗兰克把落进对方手里的前后经过，简略讲了一遍。

===Original===
"When I found myself in their hands," he said, "I decided to play possum for a while. The car was moving at incredible speed, remembering your stringent traffic regulations,"—he smiled—"and I knew that any attempt to escape on my part would result in serious injury to myself. They made no bones about their intentions. Before we were clear of London they had pulled the blinds, and one of them had switched on the electric lamp. They were both masked, and were, I think, foreigners. One sat opposite to me, all through the night, a revolver on his knees, and he did not make any disguise of his intention of employing his weapon if I gave the slightest trouble.

"I could not tell, because of the lowered blinds, which direction we were taking, but presently we struck the country and they let down one of the windows without raising the blind and I could smell the sweet scent of the fields, and knew we were miles away from London.

"I think I must have dozed a little, for very suddenly, it seemed, daylight came, and I had the good sense in waking to make as little stir as possible. I found the man sitting opposite was also in a mild doze, and the other at my side was nodding.

===Chinese===
“等我发觉自己落入他们手中，”他说，“便打定主意先装一阵子死。车子开得快得离谱——想想你们那些严苛的交通规章吧，”他笑了笑——“我知道，我这边但凡想逃，准得落个重伤的下场。他们的用意毫不遮掩。还没出伦敦，他们就把遮帘拉了下来，其中一个还拧亮了电灯。两人都蒙着面，我想是外国人。其中一个整夜坐在我对面，膝上横着一把左轮枪，而且毫不掩饰：我只要稍有异动，他就动家伙。

“遮帘拉着，我辨不出我们走的是哪个方向；不多时车子驶进了乡间，他们放下了一扇车窗，却没有卷起遮帘，我闻到田野清甜的气息，便知道我们已离伦敦好几英里了。

“我想我准是打了一会儿盹，因为天光仿佛来得非常突然。醒来时我还算机灵，尽量没弄出一点动静。只见对面那人也正迷迷糊糊打着瞌睡，我身旁的那个更是脑袋一点一点的。

===Original===
"I took a very careful survey of the situation. The car was moving very slowly, and evidently the driver had orders to move at no particular pace through the night, in order to economize the petrol. There was an inside handle to each of the doors, and I had to make up my mind by which I was to make my escape. I decided upon the near side. Gathering up my energies for one supreme effort, I suddenly leapt up, flung open the door, and jumped out. I had enough experience of the London traffic to clear the car without stumbling.

"I found myself upon a heath, innocent of any cover, save for a belt of trees about half a mile ahead of me as I ran. Fortunately the down, which was apparently flat, was, in fact, of a rolling character, and in two minutes I must have been out of sight of the car—long before they had brought the driver, himself half asleep probably, to an understanding that I had made my escape. They caught sight of me as I came up from the hollow, and one of them must have fired at me, for I heard the whistle of a bullet pass my head. That is all the story I have to tell. It was rather a tame conclusion to what promised to be a most sensational adventure."

===Chinese===
“我把形势细细掂量了一番。车子开得很慢，显见得司机得了吩咐，夜里不必赶路，省着点汽油。每扇门内侧都有把手，我得拿定主意从哪扇门脱身。我选了靠边的那扇。我把浑身的力气攒作孤注一掷，猛地纵身而起，甩开车门，跳了出去。我在伦敦的车流里练出的身手，足以让我跳车时立稳脚跟，不至于栽倒。

“我落脚的地方是一片荒野，毫无遮拦，只有我奔跑的前方约半英里处横着一带树。幸而那片丘原看似平坦，实则有起有伏，不出两分钟，我多半已逃出了他们的视线——早在他家司机——多半自己也半梦半醒——弄明白我逃了之前好久。等我从洼地里冒头时，他们瞥见了我，其中一个准是朝我开了枪，因为我听见一颗子弹贴着我脑袋呼啸而过。我要讲的就是这些。一桩眼看要惊天动地的奇遇，落到这么个不痛不痒的收场，倒也乏味。”

===Original===
At the invitation of the Reverend Jeremiah he drove back to the rectory, and left T. B. to continue his search for the missing Lady Constance. No better result attended the second scrutiny of the rooms than had resulted from the first.

"The only suggestion I can make now," said T. B., helplessly, "is that whilst our friend the coachman was reading, his lady slipped out without attracting his attention and strolled away; she will in all probability be awaiting us at the rectory."

Yet in his heart he knew that this view was absolutely wrong. The locked doors, the evidence of a struggle in the room, the bloody hand print, all pointed conclusively to foul play.

"At any rate Lady Constance Dex is somewhere within the radius of four miles," he said, grimly, "and I will find her if I have to pull down the Secret House stone by stone."

===Chinese===
应杰里迈亚牧师之邀，他乘车回了牧师住宅，留下 T. B. 继续寻访失踪的康斯坦丝夫人。第二遍细查房间的结果，同第一遍一样一无所获。

“眼下我唯一能想到的解释，”T. B. 无可奈何地说，“是咱们那位车夫朋友读书的当口，他家女主人悄没声地溜了出去，信步走开了；十有八九，她正在牧师住宅等着我们呢。”

可他心里明白，这看法错得离谱。上了锁的门、屋里搏斗的痕迹、那只血手印，桩桩件件都指向了罪行。

“无论如何，康斯坦丝·德克斯夫人就在方圆四英里之内，”他狠声说，“哪怕要我把秘密之家一块石头一块石头地拆掉，我也要找到她。”

## XVI / XVI

===Original===
The morning of Doris Gray's wedding dawned fair and bright, and she sat by the window which overlooked the gardens in Brakely Square, her hands clasped across her knees, her mind in a very tangle of confusion. It was happy for her (she argued) that there were so many considerations attached to this wedding that she had not an opportunity of thinking out, logically and to its proper end, the consequence of this act of hers.

She had had a wire from Frank on the night previous, and to her surprise it had been dated from Great Bradley. For some reason which she could not define she was annoyed that he could leave London, and be so absorbed in his work on the eve of his wedding. She gathered that his presence in that town had to do with his investigations in the Tollington case. She thought that at least he might have spent one day near her in case she wished to consult him. He took much for granted, she thought petulantly. Poltavo, on the contrary, had been most assiduous in his attention. He had had tea with her the previous afternoon, and with singular delicacy had avoided any reference to the forthcoming marriage or to his own views on the subject. But all that he did not speak, he looked. He conveyed the misery in which he stood with subtle suggestion. She felt sorry for him, had no doubt of the genuineness of his affection, or his disinterestedness. A profitable day for Poltavo in ordinary circumstances.

===Chinese===
多丽丝·格雷成婚的这个早晨，天色晴朗明媚。她坐在俯瞰布雷克利广场花园的窗边，双手交叠在膝上，心里乱作一团。这桩婚事牵扯的思虑竟这么多，倒也是她的幸运——她这样宽解自己——省得她有机会把这桩举动的前因后果，合乎逻辑地、彻头彻尾地想个透。

前一天晚上她收到弗兰克的一封电报，出乎意料，电报竟是从大布拉德利发出的。不知怎的，说不出个所以然，她心里有气：婚期前夕，他居然能离开伦敦，一头扎进他的工作里。她揣想，他去那地方，多半是为了查托灵顿家那桩案子。她觉得，他至少总该在她身边待上一天，万一她要找他商量呢。他也太想当然了，她赌气地想。波尔塔沃倒是殷勤备至。前一天下午他来陪她喝了茶，而且以其罕有的分寸，绝口不提这桩迫近的婚事，也不提他自己对这件事的看法。可是他不说出口的，全从眼睛里流露出来。他以含而不露的暗示，传达着自己所处的痛苦。她为他难过，不怀疑他感情的真切，也不怀疑他的无私。在寻常境况下，这一天对波尔塔沃算是颇有进账。

===Original===
A maid brought her from her reverie to the practical realities of life.

"Mr. Debenham has called, miss," said the girl. "I have shown him into the drawing-room."

"Mr. Debenham?" repeated Doris, with a puzzled frown. "Oh, yes, the lawyer; I will come down to him."

She found the staid solicitor walking up and down the drawing-room abstractedly.

===Chinese===
一个女仆把她从遐想中唤回现实。

“德本汉先生（Mr. Debenham）来访，小姐，”女仆说，“我把他请进客厅了。”

“德本汉先生？”多丽丝蹙着眉，困惑地重复了一遍，“哦，对了，是那位律师；我这就下去见他。”

她看见那位老成持重的律师，正在客厅里心不在焉地踱来踱去。

===Original===
"I suppose you know that I shall be a necessary guest at your wedding," he said, as he shook hands. "I have to deliver to you the keys of your uncle's safe at the London Safe Deposit. I have a memorandum here of the exact amount of money which should be in that safe."

He laid the paper on the table.

"You can look at the items at your leisure, but roughly it amounts to eight hundred thousand pounds, which was left you by your late father, who, I understand, died when you were a child."

She nodded.

"That sum is in gilt-edged securities, and you will probably find that a number of dividends are due to you. The late Mr. Farrington, when he made his arrangements for your future, chose this somewhat unusual and bizarre method of protecting your money, much against my will. I might tell you," he went on, "that he consulted me about six years ago on the subject, and I strongly advised him against it. As it happened, I was wrong, for immediately afterwards, as his books show, he must have suffered enormous losses, and although I make no suggestion against his character,"—he raised his hand deprecatingly—"yet I do say that the situation which was created by the slump in Canadian Pacifics of which he was a large holder, might very easily have tempted a man not so strong-willed as Mr. Farrington. At the present moment," he went on, "I have no more to do than discharge my duty, and I have called beforehand to see you and to ask whether your uncle spoke of the great Tollington fortune of which he was one of the trustees, though as I believe—as I know, in fact—he never handled the money."

===Chinese===
“想必您知道，您婚礼上我是少不了的客人。”他一边握手一边说，“我得把您叔叔存在伦敦保险寄存行那只保险柜的钥匙交给您。我这儿还有一份备忘单，写着那只保险柜里应有款项的确切数目。”

他把那张纸放在桌上。

“您可以闲时再细看各项，大致算来，总数达八十万英镑，是您已故父亲留给您的——据我所知，他去世时您还是个孩子。”

她点点头。

“这笔钱都换成了金边证券，您多半还会发现有好几笔到期未领的股息。已故的法林顿先生为您日后作安排时，选了这么一个相当不寻常、近乎古怪的法子来保全您的钱财，实在有违我的本意。不妨告诉您，”他接着说，“大约六年前他为这事征询过我，我当时极力劝他别这么办。结果倒是我错了，因为从他的账目看，此后不久他必是蒙受了巨额亏损；尽管我对他的为人毫无微词，”他摆了摆手，作了个告罪的姿态，“可我还是要说，他重仓持有的加拿大太平洋铁路股票一落千丈，那种局面，换一个意志不如法林顿先生坚定的人，是极容易受引诱的。眼下，”他接着说，“我只要尽到本分就行了；我提前来见您，是想问一问，您叔叔可曾提起过托灵顿那笔巨产——他是受托人之一，不过据我所信——事实上我确知——他从未经手过那笔钱。”

===Original===
She looked surprised.

"It is curious that you should ask that," she said. "Mr. Doughton is engaged in searching for the heir to that fortune."

Debenham nodded.

"So I understand," he said. "I ask because I received a communication from the other trustees in America, and I am afraid your future husband's search will be unavailing unless he can produce the heir within the next forty-eight hours."

"Why is that?" she asked in surprise.

===Chinese===
她面露惊讶。

“真巧，您偏偏问起这个，”她说，“道顿先生正受托寻找那笔财产的继承人呢。”

德本汉点点头。

“这我听说了，”他说，“我问这个，是因为我接到美国那边其他受托人的来函；恐怕您未来丈夫的寻访将徒劳无功，除非他能在四十八小时内把继承人找出来。”

“那是为什么？”她诧异地问。

===Original===
"The terms of the will are peculiar," said Mr. Debenham, walking up and down as he spoke. "The Tollington fortune, as you may know—"

"I know nothing about it," she interrupted.

"Then I will tell you." He smiled. "The fortune descends to the heir and to his wife in equal proportions."

"Suppose he is not blessed with a wife?" She smiled with something like her old gaiety.

"In that case the money automatically goes to the woman the heir eventually marries. But the terms of the will are that the heir shall be discovered within twenty years of the date of Tollington's death. The time of grace expires tomorrow."

"Poor Frank," she said, shaking her head, "and he is working so hard with his clues! I suppose if he does not produce that mysterious individual by tomorrow there will be no reward for him?"

===Chinese===
“那份遗嘱的条款很特别，”德本汉先生说着，又踱起步来，“托灵顿那笔财产，您也许知道——”

“我一无所知。”她打断道。

“那我就讲给您听。”他微笑道，“这笔财产由继承人和他的妻子对半均分。”

“要是他没那份福气娶到妻子呢？”她笑着，透出几分往日的轻快。

“那样的话，这笔钱就自动归继承人日后娶的那个女人。可遗嘱的条款规定，必须在托灵顿死后二十年之内找到继承人。宽限的期限明天就到了。”

“可怜的弗兰克，”她摇着头说，“他还在拿着那些线索拼命追呢！我想，要是明天之前他交不出那位神秘的先生，酬金就要泡汤了吧？”

===Original===
The lawyer shook his head.

"I should hardly think it likely," he said, "because the reward is for the man who complies with the conditions of the will within a stipulated time. It was because I knew Mr. Doughton had some interest in it, and because also"—he hesitated—"I thought that your uncle might have taken you into his confidence."

"That he might have told me who this missing person was, and that he himself knew; and for some reason suppressed the fact?" she asked, quickly. "Is that what you suggest, Mr. Debenham?"

"Please do not be angry with me," said the lawyer, quickly; "I do not wish to say anything against Mr. Farrington; but I know he was a very shrewd and calculating man, and I thought possibly that he might have taken you that much into his confidence, and that you might be able to help your future husband a part of the way to a very large sum of money."

She shook her head again.

"I have absolutely no knowledge of the subject. My uncle never took me into his confidence," she said; "he was very uncommunicative where business was concerned—although I am sure he was fond of me." Her eyes filled with tears, not at the recollection of his kindness, but at the humiliation she experienced at playing a part in which she had no heart. It made her feel inexpressibly mean and small.

===Chinese===
律师摇了摇头。

“我看多半没指望了，”他说，“因为酬金只给在规定期限内满足遗嘱条件的人。我来问，一是因为我知道道顿先生在其中有些利益，二来也是——”他迟疑了一下——“我以为，您叔叔或许向您吐露过内情。”

“您是说他也许告诉过我这个失踪的人是谁，说他自己知情，却出于某种缘由瞒下了这件事？”她急忙问，“您是这个意思吗，德本汉先生？”

“请别生我的气，”律师连忙说，“我不愿说法林顿先生任何坏话；可我知道他是个非常精明、工于心计的人。我想，他说不定真把这一层向您交了底，您呢，兴许能帮您未来的丈夫一把，让他朝那笔巨款走近一段路。”

她又摇了摇头。

“这件事我一无所知。我叔叔从不对我吐露什么，”她说，“凡是生意上的事，他嘴一向很严——尽管我确信他是疼我的。”她眼里涌上了泪水——不是想起了他的慈爱，而是因为她在扮演一个毫无真心的角色，心里感到的羞辱。这让她觉得自己说不出的卑微渺小。

===Original===
"That is all," said Mr. Debenham. "I shall see you at the registrar's office."

She nodded.

"May I express the hope," he said, in his heavy manner, "that your life will be a very happy one, and that your marriage will prove all you hope it will be?"

"I hardly know what I hope it will be," she said wearily, as she accompanied him to the door.

That good man shook his head sadly as he made his way back to his office.

Was there ever so unromantic and prosaic affair as this marriage, thought Doris, as she stepped into the taxicab which was to convey her to the registrar's office? She had had her dreams, as other girls had had, of that wonderful day when with pealing of the organ she would walk up the aisle perhaps upon the arm of Gregory Farrington, to a marriage which would bring nothing but delight and happiness. And here was the end of her dreams, a great heiress and a beautiful girl rocking across London in a hired cab to a furtive marriage.

===Chinese===
“就这些了，”德本汉先生说，“登记处再见。”

她点点头。

“请容我祝愿，”他一本正经地说，“愿您一生美满幸福，愿您的婚姻事事如您所愿。”

“我自己都说不清愿它是什么样。”她倦怠地说着，送他到门口。

这位好人一路走回办公室，难过地摇着头。

天底下可曾有过这样毫无诗意、乏味透顶的婚事？多丽丝登上送她去登记处的出租车时想。她也像别的姑娘一样做过梦，梦见那个美妙的日子：管风琴的乐声轰鸣，她也许挽着格雷戈里·法林顿的手臂，走过教堂的通道，走向一段只会带来欢乐与幸福的姻缘。如今，她的梦就这样收了场——一位豪富的女继承人、一个美丽的姑娘，坐在雇来的出租车里，颠簸着穿过伦敦，去赴一场偷偷摸摸的婚礼。

===Original===
Frank was waiting for her on the pavement outside the grimy little office. Mr. Debenham was there, and a clerk he had brought with him as witness. The ceremony was brief and uninteresting; she became Mrs. Doughton before she quite realized what was happening.

"There is only one thing to do now," said the lawyer as they stood outside again on the sunlit pavement.

He looked at his watch.

"We had best go straight away to the London Safe Deposit, and, if you will give me the authority, I will take formal possession of your fortune and place it in the hands of my bankers. I think these things had better be done regularly."

The girl acquiesced.

Frank was singularly silent during the drive; save to make some comment upon the amount of traffic in the streets, he did not speak to her and she was grateful for his forbearance. Her mind was in a turmoil; she was married—that was all she knew—married to somebody she liked but did not love. Married to a man who had been chosen for her partly against her will. She glanced at him out of the corners of her eyes; if she was joyless, no less was he. It was an inauspicious beginning to a married life which would end who knew how? Before the depressing granite façade of the London Safe Deposit the party descended, Mr. Debenham paid the cabman, and they went down the stone steps into the vaults of the repository.

===Chinese===
弗兰克在那间灰扑扑的小办公室外的人行道上等她。德本汉先生也到了，还带来了一个书记员充当证人。仪式简短而索然无味；她还没弄明白是怎么回事，就已经成了道顿太太。

“现在只剩一件事要办了，”他们重新站到洒满阳光的人行道上时，律师说。

他看了看表。

“咱们最好这就去伦敦保险寄存行；只要您授权给我，我就正式接管您的财产，把它存进我的银行。这些事，我看还是按规矩办妥为好。”

姑娘默许了。

一路上弗兰克出奇地沉默；除了就街上车流的多少说上两句，他没有同她讲话，她倒感激他的克制。她心里乱糟糟的；她结婚了——她知道的仅此而已——嫁给了一个她喜欢却不爱的人。嫁给了一个多少违背她本人意愿、替她选定的男人。她用眼角瞥他；她若无欢喜，他也一样不轻松。一段谁也不知结局如何的婚姻生活，就这样起了个不吉利的头。一行人在伦敦保险寄存行那座阴沉的花岗岩门面前下了车，德本汉先生付了车钱，他们便顺着石阶走下去，进了保管库的地下库房。

===Original===
There was a brief check whilst Mr. Debenham explained his authority for the visit, and it was when the officials were making reference to their books that the party was augmented by the arrival of Poltavo.

He bowed over the girl's hand, holding it a little longer than Frank could have liked, murmured colourless congratulations and nodded to Debenham.

"Count Poltavo is here, I may say," explained the lawyer, "by your late uncle's wishes. They were contained in a letter he wrote to me a few days before he disappeared."

Frank nodded grudgingly; still he was generous enough to realize something of this man's feelings if he loved Doris, and he made an especial effort to be gracious to the newcomer.

A uniformed attendant led them through innumerable corridors till they came to a private vault guarded by stout bars. The attendant opened these and they walked into a little stone chamber, illuminated by overhead lights.

The only article of furniture in the room was a small safe which stood in one corner. A very small safe indeed, thought Frank, to contain so large a fortune. The lawyer turned the key in the lock methodically, and the steel door swung back. The back of Mr. Debenham obscured their view of the safe's interior. Then he turned with an expression of wonder.

===Chinese===
德本汉先生说明此行权限时，有一番简短的查验；正当职员们翻查簿册的当口，波尔塔沃到了，一行人又添了一位。

他躬身执起姑娘的手行礼，握着不放的时间比弗兰克乐意的略长了些，嘴里咕哝了几句淡而无味的贺词，又向德本汉点了点头。

“可以说，波尔塔沃伯爵到场，”律师解释道，“是遵您已故叔叔的意愿。那意愿写在他失踪前几天给我的一封信里。”

弗兰克勉强点了点头；不过他到底气量宽宏，能够体谅这人倘若真爱多丽丝，心里会是何滋味，于是格外客气地招呼这位新来的人。

一名穿制服的管理员领着他们穿过数不清的走廊，来到一座用粗铁栏护卫的私人库房前。管理员打开铁栏，他们走进一间小小的石室，头顶的灯光照着。

屋里唯一的家具，是立在一角的一只小保险柜。这么小的一只柜子，弗兰克心想，居然装得下那么大一笔财产。律师有条不紊地把钥匙插进锁孔一拧，钢门应手而开。德本汉先生的背影挡住了他们看柜内的视线。随后他转过身来，满脸惊奇。

===Original===
"There is nothing here," he said.

"Nothing!" gasped Doris.

"Save this," said the lawyer.

He took a small envelope and handed it to the girl. She opened it mechanically and read:

"I have, unfortunately, found it necessary to utilize your fortune for the furtherance of my plans. You must try and forgive me for this; but I have given you a greater one than you have lost, a husband."

She looked up.

===Chinese===
“这里面什么也没有。”他说。

“什么也没有！”多丽丝倒抽一口气。

“除了这个。”律师说。

他取出一个小信封，递给姑娘。她机械地拆开，读到：

“我不幸发现，有必要把您的财产挪作推进我计划之用。请您务必设法原谅我；我给您的，比您失去的更为贵重——一位丈夫。”

她抬起头来。

===Original===
"What does this mean?" she whispered.

Frank took the letter from her hand and concluded the reading.

"A husband in Frank Doughton. …"

The words swam before his eyes.

"And Frank Doughton is the heir to the Tollington millions, as his father was before him. All the necessary proofs to establish his identity will be discovered in the sealed envelope which the lawyer holds, and which is inscribed 'C.'"

The letter was signed "Gregory Farrington."

===Chinese===
“这是什么意思？”她喃喃地问。

弗兰克从她手里接过信，把信读完。

“一位丈夫，弗兰克·道顿……”

这些字句在他眼前浮动。

“而弗兰克·道顿，正如他的父亲从前一样，是托灵顿百万家产的继承人。确立他身份的一切必要证明，都在律师手中那只封套里，封套上标着一个‘C’字。”

信的署名是“格雷戈里·法林顿”。

===Original===
The lawyer was the first to recover his self-possession; his practical mind went straight to the business at hand.

"There is such an envelope in my office," he said, "given to me by Mr. Farrington with strict instructions that it was not to be handed to his executors or to any person until definite instructions arrived—instructions which would be accompanied by unmistakable proof as to the necessity for its being handed over. I congratulate you, Mr. Doughton."

He turned and shook hands with the bewildered Frank, who had been listening like a man in a dream; the heir to the Tollington millions; he, the son of George Doughton, and all the time he had been looking for—what? For his own grandmother!

It came on him all of a rush. He knew now that all his efforts, all his search might have been saved, if he had only realized the Christian name of his father's mother.

He had only the dimmest recollection of the placid-faced lady who had died whilst he was at school; he had never associated in his mind this serene old lady, who had passed away only a few hours before her beloved husband, with the Annie for whom he had searched. It made him gasp—then he came to earth quickly as he realized that his success had come with the knowledge of his wife's financial ruin. He looked at her as she stood there—it was too vast a shock for her to realize at once.

He put his arm about her shoulder, and Poltavo, twirling his little moustache, looked at the two through his lowered lids with an ugly smile playing at the corner of his mouth.

===Chinese===
律师第一个回过神来；他那颗务实的头脑立刻转到眼前的正事上。

“我事务所里确实有这么一只封套，”他说，“是法林顿先生交给我的，还下了严格的指令：非等明确的吩咐到来，不得交给他的遗嘱执行人，也不得交给任何人——而且那吩咐必须伴有无可置疑的凭据，证明它非交出不可。恭喜您，道顿先生。”

他转过身，同那个听得分不清东南西北的弗兰克握手——弗兰克一直恍恍惚惚如在梦中：托灵顿百万家产的继承人；他，乔治·道顿的儿子；而他一直以来苦苦寻找的，竟是——什么？竟是他自己的祖母！

这一切猛地涌上他的心头。他现在才明白，自己所有的奔忙、所有的寻访原都可以省去，只要他早一点想起他父亲的母亲的名字。

他对那位在他上学期间去世的、面容安详的夫人，只剩一点极模糊的记忆；他从未在心里把这位娴静的老太太——她只比她深爱的丈夫早走几个钟头——同他寻访多时的那个安妮联想到一处。这让他倒抽一口冷气——随即他猛地跌回现实：他的成功，是伴着妻子财产尽失的消息一同到来的。他望着站在那儿的她——这打击太大，她一时还无法完全领会。

他揽住她的肩；波尔塔沃捻着小胡子，垂着眼皮打量这两人，嘴角挂着一丝阴笑。

===Original===
"It is all right, dear," said Frank soothingly; "your money is secure—it was only a temporary use he made of it."

"It is not that," she said, with a catch in her throat; "it is the feeling that my uncle trapped you into this marriage. I did not mind his dissipating my own fortune; the money is nothing to me. But he has caught you by a trick, and he has used me as a bait." She covered her face with her hands.

In a few moments she had composed herself; she spoke no other word, but suffered herself to be led out of the building into the waiting cab. Poltavo watched them drive off with that fierce little smile of his, and turned to the lawyer.

"A clever man, Mr. Farrington," he said, in a bitter tone of reluctant admiration.

The lawyer looked at him steadily.

"His Majesty's prisons are filled with men who specialize in that kind of cleverness," he said, drily, and left Poltavo without another word.

===Chinese===
“没事的，亲爱的，”弗兰克宽慰道，“你的钱是稳的——他只是一时借用罢了。”

“不是为这个，”她说，喉咙里哽了一下，“是因为我觉得，是叔叔设圈套把你骗进了这桩婚事。他败光我自己的财产，我并不放在心上；钱财对我算不得什么。可他用诡计网住了你，还拿我当了饵。”她双手捂住了脸。

不多时她便镇定下来；她再没说一句话，任人领出大楼，上了候着的车。波尔塔沃带着他那狞厉的浅笑，目送车子开走，然后转向律师。

“一个聪明人，这位法林顿先生。”他说，语气里带着不得不服的酸涩。

律师定定地看着他。

“陛下的大小监狱里，关满了精通这种聪明的能人。”他冷冷地说完，丢下波尔塔沃，再无一言。

## XVII / XVII

===Original===
T. B. Smith was playing a round of golf at Walton Heath, when the news was telephoned through to him.

He left immediately for town, and picked up Ela at luncheon at the Fritz Hotel, where the detective had his headquarters.

"The whole thing is perfectly clear, now," he said. "The inexplicable disappearance of Mr. Farrington is explained in poster type, 'that he who runs may read.'"

"I am a little hazy about the solution myself," said Ela dubiously.

===Chinese===
消息电话传来时，T. B. 史密斯正在沃尔顿希思（Walton Heath）打一轮高尔夫。

他立刻动身进城，到弗里茨酒店（Fritz Hotel）寻着了正在用午餐的埃拉——侦探的总部就设在那家酒店。

“现在，整件事一清二楚了，”他说，“法林顿先生那桩不可解的失踪，已经用海报体的大字写明白了——‘凡奔跑之人皆可读到’。”

“我自己对这答案，倒还有点云里雾里。”埃拉将信将疑地说。

===Original===
"Then I will put it in plain language for you," said T. B. as he speared a sardine from the hors d'oeuvre dish. "Farrington knew all along that the heir to the Tollington millions was George Doughton. He knew it years and years ago, and it was for that reason he settled at Great Bradley, where the Doughtons had their home. Evidently the two older Doughtons were dead at this time, and only George Doughton, the romantic and altogether unpractical explorer, represented the family.

"George was in love with the lady who is now known as Lady Constance Dex, and knowing this, Farrington evidently took every step that was possible to ingratiate himself into her good graces. He knew that the fortune would descend equally to Doughton and to his wife. Doughton was a widower and had a son, a youngster at the time, and it is very possible that, the boy being at school, and being very rarely in Great Bradley, Farrington had no idea of his existence.

"The knowledge that this boy was alive must have changed all his plans; at any rate, the engagement was allowed to drift on, whilst he matured some scheme whereby he could obtain a large portion of the Tollington millions for his own use. Again I think his plans must have been changed.

"It was whilst he was at Great Bradley that he was entrusted with the guardianship of Doris Gray, and as his affection for the young girl grew—an affection which I think was one of the few wholesome things in his life—he must have seen the extraordinary chance which fate had placed in his way.

===Chinese===
“那我就用大白话讲给你听。”T. B. 说着，从冷盘里叉起一条沙丁鱼。“法林顿从头到尾都知道，托灵顿百万家产的继承人，是乔治·道顿。这他早在许多许多年前就知道了，也正为这个缘故，他才在大布拉德利安了家——道顿家的老宅就在那儿。显见得那时道顿家老一辈的两位都已不在人世，代表这一族的，只剩乔治·道顿——那位浪漫而全然不切实际的探险家。

“乔治爱上了如今人称康斯坦丝·德克斯夫人的那位女士；法林顿知道这一点，显然便使出浑身解数，百般博她的欢心。他知道那笔财产将由道顿和他的妻子均分。道顿是个鳏夫，有个儿子，当时还是个小孩子；很可能因为这孩子常年住在学校，难得回大布拉德利，法林顿根本不知道有这么个人。

“后来知道这孩子还活着，他的全盘计划必是为之大变；不管怎么说，那桩婚约就那么悬着，任其拖延，他则从从容容酝酿着某个方案，好把托灵顿百万家产的一大块据为己用。再后来，我看他的计划又不得不再次变更。

“正是住在大布拉德利期间，他受托做了多丽丝·格雷的监护人；随着他对这年轻姑娘的感情与日俱增——我认为这份感情，是他一生中少有的几样健全的东西之一——他必定看出了命运送到他手边的这个千载难逢的机会。

===Original===
"With diabolical ingenuity and with a remorselessness which is reminiscent of the Borgias he planned first George Doughton's death, and then the bringing together of Doughton's son and his own ward. There is every proof of this to be found in his subsequent actions. He was prepared to introduce the young people to one another, and by affording them opportunities for meeting, and such encouragement as he could give, to bring about the result he so desired.

"But things did not move fast enough for him, and then he must have learnt, as the other trustees seem to have learnt recently, that there was an undiscovered time limit. He threw out hints to his niece, hints which were received rather coldly. He had taken the bold step of employing Frank Doughton to discover—himself! That was a move which had a twofold purpose. It kept the young man in contact with him. It also satisfied the other trustees, who had entrusted to Farrington the task of employing the necessary measures to discover the missing heir.

"But neither hint nor suggestion served him. The girl's fortune was due for delivery to her care, and his guardianship expired almost at the same time as the time limit for discovery of the Tollington millionaire came to an end. He had to take a desperate step; there were other reasons, of course, contributing to his move.

"The knowledge that he was suspected by me, the certainty that Lady Constance Dex would betray him, once she discovered that he had sent her lover to his death, all these were contributing factors, but the main reason for his disappearance was the will that was read after his bogus death.

"In that will he conveyed unchallengeable instructions for the girl to marry Frank Doughton without delay. I suspect that the girl now knows he is alive. Probably, panic-stricken by her tardiness, he has disclosed his hand so far as the alleged death is concerned."

===Chinese===
“他凭着魔鬼般的机巧、凭着叫人想起波吉亚家族（Borgias）的狠辣无情，先谋划了乔治·道顿的死，再谋划让道顿的儿子同他自己的被监护人结好。他后来的种种行径，处处可以找到证据。他本来就打算引荐这两个年轻人相识，再给他们制造见面的机会，给他们力所能及的鼓励，促成他梦寐以求的结局。

“可是事情进展得不够快；后来他想必同其他受托人最近才发觉的一样，发觉了还有一个不为人知的期限。他向侄女递过话，那些暗示却被相当冷淡地接了过去。他走了大胆的一步：雇了弗兰克·道顿去寻找——他自己！这一着棋一箭双雕。一来把那年轻人拴在自己身边；二来也应付了其他受托人——他们正是把设法寻找失踪继承人这件事托付给法林顿的。

“可暗示也罢，提醒也罢，都不管用。姑娘的财产就要交到她自己手里，他的监护权几乎同寻找托灵顿百万富翁的期限一道到期。他只好铤而走险；当然，促成他这一动的，还有别的缘故。

“他知道我盯上了他；他也断得准，康斯坦丝·德克斯夫人一旦查明是她送她的情人去死的，必定饶不了他——这些都推了他一把；可他失踪的主因，还是他那场假死之后宣读的那份遗嘱。

“那份遗嘱里，他给姑娘下了不容抗辩的指令：立刻嫁给弗兰克·道顿，不得耽搁。我疑心那姑娘如今已经知道他还活着。多半是被她的拖延吓慌了，他在‘所谓死亡’这一节上，已经向她摊了牌。”

===Original===
T. B. looked out of the window on to the stream of life which was flowing east and west along Piccadilly; his face was set in a little frown of doubt and anxiety.

"I can take Farrington tomorrow if I want to," he said after a moment, "but I wish to gather up every string of organization in my hands."

"What of Lady Constance Dex?" asked Ela. "Whilst we are waiting, she is in some little danger."

T. B. shook his head.

"If she is not dead now," he said simply, "she will be spared. If Farrington wished to kill her—for Farrington it was who spirited her away—he could have done so in the house; no one would have been any the wiser as to the murderer. Lady Constance must wait; we must trust to luck before I inspect that underground chamber of which I imagine she is at present an unwilling inmate. I want to crush this blackmailing force," he said, thumping the table with energy; "I want to sweep out of England the whole organization which is working right under the nose of the police and in defiance of all laws; and until I have done that, I shall not sleep soundly in my bed."

===Chinese===
T. B. 望向窗外皮卡迪利大街（Piccadilly）上东西川流的人潮；他的眉头微微蹙起，透着疑虑与焦灼。

“只要我愿意，明天就能拿住法林顿，”过了一会儿他说，“可我要把这张网的每一根线，都先攥进自己手心。”

“那康斯坦丝·德克斯夫人怎么办？”埃拉问，“咱们这么等着，她可多少有点危险。”

T. B. 摇了摇头。

“她这会儿要是还没死，”他平平地说，“那就会留她一命。法林顿若想杀她——把她劫走的正是法林顿——在屋里就能动手，谁也不会对凶手多知道半分。康斯坦丝夫人只能等；在去查那间地下密室之前——我料想她眼下正身不由己地被关在那里——我们只能听天由命。我要捣碎这股敲诈势力，”他用力捶着桌子说，“我要把整个这张就在警察鼻子底下、目无法纪地运转着的网，从英格兰一扫而光；做不到这一步，我夜里睡觉都不踏实。”

===Original===
"And Poltavo?"

"Poltavo," smiled T. B., "can wait for just a little while."

He paid the bill and the two men passed out of the hotel and crossed Piccadilly. A man who had been lounging along apparently studying the shop windows saw them out of the corner of his eye and followed them carelessly. Another man, no less ostentatiously reading a newspaper, as he walked along the pavement on the opposite side of the thoroughfare, followed close behind.

T. B. and his companion turned into Burlington Arcade and reached Cork Street. Save for one or two pedestrians the street was utterly deserted, and the first of the shadowers quickened his pace. He put his hand in his tail pocket and took out something which glinted in the April sunlight, but before he could raise his hand the fourth man, now on his heels, dropped his newspaper, and flinging one arm around the shadower's neck, and placing his knee in the small of the other's back, wrenched the pistol away with his disengaged hand.

===Chinese===
“那波尔塔沃呢？”

“波尔塔沃嘛，”T. B. 微微一笑，“还可以再等一等。”

他付了账，两人出了酒店，穿过皮卡迪利大街。一个一路闲荡、貌似端详橱窗的人，用眼角瞥见他们，便若无其事地跟了上来。另一个人毫不逊色，煞有介事地边走边读报纸，沿着大街对面的人行道，紧紧缀在后头。

T. B. 和同伴拐进伯灵顿拱廊（Burlington Arcade），走到科克街（Cork Street）。街上除了寥寥一两个行人，空空荡荡；头一个盯梢的加快了脚步。他把手伸进后兜，掏出一件在四月阳光下闪着寒光的东西；可他手还没来得及抬起，第四个人已经到了他背后，丢下报纸，一条臂膀勒住盯梢者的脖颈，膝盖顶住他的后腰，腾出的那只手把他手里的手枪夺了下来。

===Original===
T. B. turned at the sound of the struggle and came back to assist the shadowing detective. The prisoner was a little man, sharp-featured, and obviously a member of one of the great Latin branches of the human race. A tiny black moustache, fierce scowling eyebrows, and liquid brown eyes now blazing with hate, spoke of a Southern origin.

Deftly the three police officers searched and disarmed him; a pair of adjustable handcuffs snapped upon the man's thin wrists, and before the inevitable crowd could gather the prisoner and his custodians were being whirled to Vine Street in a cab.

===Chinese===
T. B. 听见搏斗声转过身，回来帮了反盯梢的侦探一把。俘虏是个小个子，五官尖削，显然属于人类拉丁支系中的某一族。一撇黑色小胡子，两道凶横紧锁的浓眉，一双此刻燃着恨火的棕色水灵眼睛，都说明他来自南方。

三名警员利落地搜了他的身、下了他的械；一副活动手铐咔嗒一声锁上那人的细手腕；不等看热闹的人群照例聚拢，俘虏和押解他的人已坐着马车，风驰电掣地去了瓦因街（Vine Street）。

===Original===
They placed the man in the steel dock and asked him the usual questions, but he maintained a dogged silence. That his object had been assassination no one could doubt, for in addition to the automatic pistol, which he had obviously intended using at short range, trusting to luck to make his escape, they found a long stiletto in his breast pocket.

More to the point, and of greater interest to T. B., there was a three-line scrawl on a piece of paper in Italian, which, translated, showed that minute instructions had been given to the would-be murderer as to T. B.'s whereabouts.

"Put him in a cell," said T. B. "I think we are going to find things out. If this is not one of Poltavo's hired thugs, I am greatly mistaken."

Whatever he was, the man offered no information which might assist the detective in his search for the truth, but maintained an unbroken silence, and T. B. gave up the task of questioning him in sheer despair.

===Chinese===
他们把那人放进铁栏被告席，照例问了几句，他却咬紧牙关一言不发。他的图谋是行刺，这一点谁也不怀疑，因为除了那支显然打算近距离使用、指望侥幸脱身的自动手枪，他们还在他胸前口袋里搜出一柄细长的匕首。

更要紧、也更叫 T. B. 上心的，是一张纸上三行意大利文的潦草字迹；译出来才见分晓：上面把 T. B. 的行踪，向这位未遂的凶手交代得一丝不漏。

“把他关进牢房，”T. B. 说，“我想咱们就要查出些名堂了。这人要不是波尔塔沃雇的打手，我把名字倒过来写。”

不管他是什么来头，这人没有吐露半点有助于查明真相的消息，只是矢口不语；T. B. 万般无奈，只得断了盘问他的念头。

===Original===
The next morning at daybreak the prisoner was aroused and told to dress. He was taken out to where a motor car was awaiting him, and a few moments later he was speeding on the way to Dover. Two detective officers placed him on a steamer and accompanied him to Calais. At Calais they took a courteous leave of him, handing him a hundred francs and the information in his own tongue that he had been deported on an order from the Home Secretary, obtained at midnight the previous night.

The prisoner took his departure with some eagerness and spent the greater portion of his hundred francs in addressing a telegram to Poltavo.

T. B. Smith, who knew that telegram would come, was sitting in the Continental instrument room of the General Post Office when it arrived. He was handed a copy of the telegram and read it. Then he smiled.

"Thank you," he said, as he passed it back to the Superintendent of the department, "this may now be transmitted for delivery. I know all I want to know."

===Chinese===
第二天清晨破晓，俘虏被叫醒，吩咐更衣。他被带出去，一辆汽车正候着他；不多时，他已在通往多佛尔（Dover）的路上飞驰。两名侦探警官把他安置上一艘轮船，一路押到加来（Calais）。在加来，他们客客气气地同他道别，递给他一百法郎，并用他的母语告诉他：他是奉内政大臣之令被驱逐出境的——那道命令是前一天半夜里办下来的。

俘虏相当急切地上了路，把那一百法郎的大半，花在了给波尔塔沃发的一封电报上。

T. B. 史密斯早料到会有这封电报；电报到时，他正坐在邮政总局的大陆电报机房里。有人递给他一份电报抄件，他读了，随即微微一笑。

“谢谢，”他把抄件还给该部门的警司，说，“现在可以放行投递了。我想知道的都知道了。”

===Original===
Poltavo received the message an hour later, and having read it, cursed his subordinate's indiscretion, for the message was in Italian, plain for everybody to read who understood that language, and its purport easy to understand for anybody who had a knowledge of the facts.

He waited all that day for a visit from the police, and when T. B. arrived in the evening Poltavo was ready with an excuse and an explanation. But neither excuse nor explanation was asked for. T. B.'s questions had to do with something quite different, namely the new Mrs. Doughton and her vanished fortune.

"I was in the confidence of Mr. Farrington," said Poltavo, relieved to find the visit had nothing to do with that which he most dreaded, "but I was amazed to discover that the safe was empty. It was a tremendous tragedy for the poor young lady. She is in Paris now with her husband," he added.

T. B. nodded.

"Perhaps you will give me their address?" he asked.

===Chinese===
一小时后波尔塔沃收到了电文，读过便骂手下人嘴不严：电文是意大利文，凡懂那语言的人都一读即知，而只要知道几分内情，其主旨也是一望即明。

他一整天都在等着警察上门；等到傍晚 T. B. 真来了，波尔塔沃早已备好了托词和说辞。可人家既没讨托词，也没讨说辞。T. B. 问的是全然另一回事——新任道顿太太，和她那不翼而飞的财产。

“法林顿先生的心腹事，我是一向与闻的，”波尔塔沃说，见这次来访同他最怕的事情毫不相干，不由松了口气，“可发现保险柜空空如也，我也大为震惊。对可怜的年轻太太，这是一场天大的悲剧。她如今同丈夫在巴黎。”他补了一句。

T. B. 点点头。

“也许您可以给我他们的地址？”他问。

===Original===
"With pleasure," said Count Poltavo, reaching for his address book.

"I may be going to Paris myself tomorrow," T. B. went on, "and I will look these young people up. I suppose it is not the correct thing for anyone to call upon honeymoon couples, but a police officer has privileges."

There was an exchange of smiles. Poltavo was almost exhilarated that T. B.'s visit had nothing to do with him personally. A respect, which amounted almost to fear, characterized his attitude toward the great Scotland Yard detective. He credited T. B. with qualities which perhaps that admirable man did not possess, but, as a set-off against this, he failed to credit him with a wiliness which was peculiarly T. B.'s chief asset. For who could imagine that the detective's chief object in calling upon Poltavo that evening was to allay his suspicions and soothe down his fears. Yet T. B. came for no other reason and with no other purpose. It was absolutely necessary that Poltavo should be taken off his guard, for T. B. was planning the coup which was to end for all time the terror under which hundreds of innocent people in England were lying.

After an exchange of commonplace civilities the two men parted—T. B., as he said, with his hand on the door, to prepare for his Paris trip, and Poltavo to take up what promised to be one of the most interesting cases that the Fallock blackmailers had ever handled.

===Chinese===
“非常乐意。”波尔塔沃伯爵说着，伸手去拿通讯录。

“我明天说不定也要去巴黎，”T. B. 接着说，“我会去看看这对年轻人。去拜访度蜜月的新婚夫妇，照礼数大约不合规矩——可警察自有警察的特权。”

两人相视一笑。T. B. 的来访与他本人无涉，波尔塔沃几乎有些飘飘然。他对这位苏格兰场大侦探的态度，是一种近乎畏惧的敬重。他认定 T. B. 身上有种种本事——那也许远超出这位可敬人物的实情；可是相抵之下，他偏偏没算到 T. B. 还有一宗狡黠，而那正是 T. B. 最要紧的本钱。因为谁能想到，这位侦探当晚造访波尔塔沃的头一桩目的，就是稳住他的疑心、抚平他的恐惧。然而 T. B. 此来别无他由、别无他图。非叫波尔塔沃放松警惕不可，因为 T. B. 正在筹划那雷霆一击——要叫英格兰成百上千无辜之人身受其害的恐怖，从此绝根。

彼此客套了几句场面话，两人分手——T. B. 如他自己所说，手已搭上门把，要去准备巴黎之行；波尔塔沃则着手经办一桩案子，一桩有望成为法洛克敲诈集团经手过的最有意思的案子。

===Original===
He waited until he heard the door close after the detective; until he had watched him, from the window, step into his cab and be whirled away, then he unlocked the lower drawer of his desk, touched a spring in the false bottom, and took from a secret recess a small bundle of letters.

Many of the sheets of notepaper which he spread out on the table before him bore the strawberry crest of his grace the Duke of Ambury. The letters were all in the same sprawling handwriting; ill-spelt and blotted, but they were very much to the point. The Duke of Ambury, in his exuberant youth, had contracted a marriage with a lady in Gibraltar. His regiment had been stationed at that fortress when his succession to the dukedom had been a very remote possibility, and the Spanish lady to whom, as the letters showed, he had plighted his troth, and to whom he was eventually married in the name of Wilson (a copy of the marriage certificate was in the drawer), had been a typical Spaniard of singular beauty and fascination, though of no distinguished birth.

Apparently his grace had regretted his hasty alliance, for two years after his succession to the title, he had married the third daughter of the Earl of Westchester without—so far as the evidence in Poltavo's possession showed—having gone through the formality of releasing himself from his previous union.

Here was a magnificent coup, the most splendid that had ever come into the vision of the blackmailers, for the Duke of Ambury was one of the richest men in England, a landlord who owned half London and had estates in almost every county. If ever there was a victim who was in a position to be handsomely bled, here was one.

===Chinese===
他等到听见门在侦探身后关上，等到从窗口望着那人登上马车、绝尘而去，这才打开书桌的下层抽屉，按动夹底的一个暗簧，从密格里取出一小捆信件。

他摊开在面前的这些信笺，许多都印着安伯里公爵（Duke of Ambury）大人家的草莓纹章。信全出自同一只手，字迹张牙舞爪，拼写错误百出、涂改斑斑，内容却句句切中要害。安伯里公爵在血气方刚的年纪，在直布罗陀（Gibraltar）同一位女士结了婚。他那个团当时驻扎在那座要塞，那时他继承公爵之位还是遥不可及的事；而那位西班牙女士——从信中可见他向她许过婚誓，后来又以威尔逊（Wilson）之名同她正式成婚（结婚证书的抄件就收在抽屉里）——是个美貌动人、极有魅力的地道西班牙人，只是出身寒微。

显见得公爵大人后来悔了这门仓促的姻缘，因为袭爵两年之后，他便娶了威斯特切斯特伯爵（Earl of Westchester）的三小姐，而且——就波尔塔沃掌握的证据来看——并未办过任何手续，解除先前那段婚姻。

好一票漂亮的买卖，敲诈者们做梦也没见过这么阔的：安伯里公爵是英格兰数一数二的豪富，半个伦敦的地皮都归他所有，几乎郡郡都有他的庄园。要论榨得出油水的肥羊，天底下再找不出比这更肥的了。

===Original===
The Spanish wife was now dead, but an heir had been born to the Duke of Ambury before the death, and the whole question of succession was affected by the threatened disclosure. All the facts of the case were in Poltavo's possession; they were written in this curiously uneducated hand which filled the pages of the letters now spread upon the table in front of him. The marriage certificate had been supplied, and a copy of the death certificate had also been obligingly extracted by a peccant servant, and matters were now so far advanced that Poltavo had received, through the Agony column of the *Times*, a reply to the demand he had sent to his victim.

===Chinese===
那位西班牙妻子如今已死，但在她死前，安伯里公爵已得了一位子嗣，于是整个继承权的问题，都系于这桩一旦揭破的隐情。案情的全部事实都在波尔塔沃手里；它们就写在那手出奇地没有教养的字迹里，涂满了此刻摊在他面前桌上的信纸。结婚证书已经到手，死亡证书的抄件也有个手脚不干净的仆人热心地弄了来；事态已进展到这一步：波尔塔沃已通过《泰晤士报》的启事栏，收到了猎物对他索求的答复。

===Original===
That reply had been very favourable; there had been no suggestion of lawyers; no hint of any intervention on the part of the police. Ambury was willing to be bled, willing indeed, so the agony advertisement indicated to Poltavo, to make any financial sacrifice in order to save the honour of his house.

It was only a question of terms now. Poltavo had decided upon fifty thousand pounds. That sum would be sufficient to enable him to clear out of England and to enjoy life as he best loved it, without the necessity for taking any further risks. With Doris Gray removed from his hands, with the approval of society already palling upon him, he thirsted for new fields and new adventures. The fifty thousand seemed now within his grasp. He should, by his agreement with Farrington, hand two-thirds of that sum to his employer, but even the possibility of his doing this never for one moment occurred to him.

Farrington, so he told himself, a man in hiding, powerless and in Poltavo's hands practically, could not strike back at him; the cards were all in favour of the Count. He had already received some ten thousand pounds as a result of his work in London, and he had frantic and ominous letters from Dr. Fall demanding that the "house" share should be forwarded without delay. These demands Poltavo had treated with contempt. He felt master of the situation, inasmuch that he had placed the major portion of the balance of money in hand, other than that which had been actually supplied by Farrington, to his own credit in a Paris bank. He was prepared for all eventualities, and here he was promised the choicest of all his pickings—for the bleeding of the Duke of Ambury would set a seal upon previous accomplishments.

===Chinese===
那答复非常爽快；没有一句提到律师，也没有一字暗示要请警察。安伯里甘愿挨宰——那则启事广告向波尔塔沃表明，他实在是甘愿倾尽钱财，只求保住家族的体面。

如今只差讲价钱了。波尔塔沃已拿定主意：五万英镑。这笔钱足够他撤离英格兰、照他最贪恋的方式享乐余生，而不必再冒半点风险。多丽丝·格雷已从他手心里脱了手，社交界的追捧也早已叫他腻烦；他渴望新的天地、新的奇遇。那五万此刻仿佛已在他掌中。按他同法林顿的约定，这笔钱他该交三分之二给雇主——可就连“交钱”这个念头，他一刹那也不曾动过。

法林顿——他对自己说——一个藏头露尾的人，无权无势，实际上就攥在他波尔塔沃手心里，还能反咬他一口？牌面张张都利于伯爵。他在伦敦的这番经营，已给他挣下约莫一万英镑；福尔医生写来一封封气急败坏、语带威胁的信，催他把“宅子”那份分成即刻汇去。这些索求，波尔塔沃一概嗤之以鼻。他自觉大局在握：除去法林顿实际拨给的那部分，手头余款的大头，他早已存进了巴黎一家银行的自己名下。他万事有备，而这厢又有全部买卖里最肥的一票等着他——榨干安伯里公爵这一票，将给他先前的种种成就盖下最后一方金印。

===Original===
He rang a bell, and a man came, letting himself into the room with a key. He was an Italian with a peculiarly repulsive face; one of the small fry whom Poltavo had employed from time to time to do such work as was beneath his own dignity, or which promised an unnecessary measure of danger in its performance.

"Carlos," said Poltavo, speaking in Italian, "Antonio has been arrested, and has been taken to Calais by the police."

"That I know, signor," nodded the man. "He is very fortunate. I was afraid when the news came that he would be put into prison."

Poltavo smiled.

"The ways of the English police are beyond understanding," he said lightly. "Here was our Antonio, anxious and willing to kill the head of the detective department, and they release him! Is it not madness? At any rate, Antonio will not be coming back, because though they are mad, the police are not so foolish as to allow him to land again. I have telegraphed to our friend to go on to Paris and await me, and here let me say, Carlos,"—he tapped the table with the end of his penholder—"that if you by ill-fortune should ever find yourself in the same position of our admirable and worthy Antonio, I beg that you will not send me telegrams."

===Chinese===
他按铃，一个男人用自己的钥匙开了门进来。他是个意大利人，长相尤其惹人厌；是波尔塔沃不时雇用的虾兵蟹将之一，干的都是些有失他自己身份的活计，或是办起来凶险过甚的差事。

“卡洛斯（Carlos），”波尔塔沃用意大利语说，“安东尼奥（Antonio）被捕了，警察把他押到加去了。”

“这我知道，先生。”那人点点头，“他运气真好。消息传来时，我还当他要坐牢呢。”

波尔塔沃微微一笑。

“英国警察的路数，真是匪夷所思，”他轻描淡写地说，“咱们的安东尼奥，一门心思想要侦探部门头子的命，他们居然放了他！这不是疯了吗？不过话说回来，安东尼奥是回不来了，因为他们纵然疯，还不至于蠢到再放他上岸。我已经打电报叫咱们那位朋友去巴黎等我。顺便说一句，卡洛斯，”他用笔杆头敲了敲桌子，“倘若你哪天不幸也落到咱们那位可敬可佩的安东尼奥的田地，我求你，千万别给我发电报。”

===Original===
"You may be assured, excellent signor," said the man with a little grin, "that I shall not send you telegrams, for I cannot write."

"A splendid deficiency," said Poltavo.

He took up a letter from the table.

"You will deliver this to a person who will meet you at the corner of Branson Square. The exact position I have already indicated to you."

===Chinese===
“您尽管放心，好先生，”那人咧嘴一笑说，“我绝不会给您发电报——因为我不识字。”

“这个毛病生得好。”波尔塔沃说。

他从桌上拿起一封信。

“你把这封信送给一个人，他会在布兰森广场（Branson Square）的拐角上等你。具体方位我已经指点过你了。”

===Original===
The man nodded.

"This person will give you in exchange another letter. You will not return to me but you will go to your brother's house in Great Saffron Street, and outside that house you will see a man standing who wears a long overcoat. You will brush past him, and in doing so you will drop this envelope into his pocket—you understand?"

"Excellency, I quite understand," said the man.

"Go, and God be with you," said the pious Poltavo, sending forth a message which he believed would bring consternation and terror into the bosom of the Duke of Ambury.

===Chinese===
那人点点头。

“那人会另给你一封信作为交换。你不要回我这儿来，径直去大萨弗龙街（Great Saffron Street）你哥哥家；那宅子门外会站着一个穿长外套的男人。你从他身边擦过，擦过的时候，把这个信封丢进他口袋——明白吗？”

“大人，小的完全明白。”那人说。

“去吧，愿上帝与你同在。”虔敬的波尔塔沃说；他发出的这封函件，他相信将在安伯里公爵大人胸中掀起惊惶与恐怖。

===Original===
It was late that night when Carlos Freggetti came down a steep declivity into Great Saffron Street and walked swiftly along that deserted thoroughfare till he came to his brother's house. His brother was a respectable Italian artisan, engaged by an asphalt company in London. Near the narrow door of the tenement in which his relative lived, a stranger stood, apparently awaiting someone. Carlos, in passing him, stumbled and apologized under his breath. At that moment he slipped the letter into the other's pocket. His quick eyes noted the identity of the stranger. It was Poltavo. No one else was in the street, and in the dim light even the keenest of eyes would not have seen the transfer of the envelope. Poltavo strolled to the end of the thoroughfare, jumped into the taxicab which was waiting and reached his house after various transferences of cabs without encountering any of T. B.'s watchful agents. In his room he opened the letter with an anxious air. Would Ambury agree to the exorbitant sum he had demanded? And if he did not agree, what sum would he be prepared to pay as the price of the blackmailer's silence? The first words brought relief to him.

> "I am willing to pay the sum you ask, although I think you are guilty of a dastardly crime," read the letter, "and since you seem to suspect my bonafides, I shall choose, as an agent to carry the money to you, an old labourer on my Lancashire estate who will be quite ignorant of the business in hand, and who will give you the money in exchange for the marriage certificate. If you will choose a rendezvous where you can meet, a rendezvous which fulfills all your requirements as to privacy, I will undertake to have my man on the spot at the time you wish."

There was a triumphant smile on Poltavo's face as he folded the letter.

===Chinese===
那晚夜深时分，卡洛斯·弗雷盖蒂（Carlos Freggetti）从一道陡坡下到大萨弗龙街，沿着那条空无一人的街道快步走去，到了他哥哥家。他哥哥是个本分的意大利工匠，受雇于伦敦一家沥青公司。亲戚住的那栋杂居楼窄门近旁，站着一个陌生人，像是在等谁。卡洛斯从他身边经过时一个趔趄，低声道了声歉。就在那一瞬，他把信塞进了对方口袋。他眼尖，认出了这陌生人的身份——正是波尔塔沃。街上再无别人，光线昏暗，纵是最利的眼睛也看不见那只信封的交接。波尔塔沃踱到街尾，跳上候着的出租车，几番换车之后回到家里，一路没有撞上 T. B. 那些耳目灵通的探员。在房间里，他神色焦灼地拆开了信。安伯里会答应他开的那个天价吗？倘若不答应，他又肯出多少，来买敲诈者的沉默？头一行字就让他放了心。

> “我愿意支付你所索的数目，尽管我认为你犯下了卑劣的罪行，”信上写道，“既然你似乎怀疑我的诚意，我便选定这样一个人为我送钱——我兰开夏（Lancashire）庄园上的一名老雇工，他对这桩交易一无所知，会一手交钱、一手向你换取那份结婚证书。你若能选定一个会面地点，一个满足你一切隐秘要求的地点，我保证让我的人在你指定的时刻到场。”

波尔塔沃折起信，脸上挂着得胜的微笑。

===Original===
"Now," he said half aloud, "now, my friend Farrington, you and I will part company. You have ceased to be of any service to me; your value has decreased in the same proportion as my desire for freedom has advanced. Fifty thousand pounds!" he repeated admiringly. "Ernesto, you have a happy time before you. All the continent of Europe is at your feet, and this sad England is behind you. Congratulations, amigo!"

The question of the rendezvous was an important one. Though he read into the letter an eagerness on the part of his victim to do anything to avoid the scandal and the exposure which Poltavo threatened, yet he did not trust him. The old farm labourer was a good idea, but where could they meet? When Poltavo had kidnapped Frank Doughton he had intended taking him to a little house he had hired in the East End of London. The journey to the Secret House was a mere blind to throw suspicion upon Farrington and to put the police off the real track. The car would have returned to London, and under the influence of a drug he had intended to smuggle Frank into the small house at West Ham, where he was to be detained until the period which Farrington had stipulated had expired.

But the transfer of money in the house was a different matter. The place could be surrounded by police. No, it must be an open space; such a space as would enable Poltavo to command a clear view on every side.

Why not Great Bradley, he thought, after a while? Again he would be serving two purposes. He would be leading the police to the Secret House, and he would have the mansion of mystery and all its resources as a refuge in case anything went wrong at the last moment. He could, in the worst extremity, explain that he was collecting the money on behalf of Farrington.

Yes, Great Bradley and the wild stretch of down on the south of the town was the place. He made his arrangements accordingly.

===Chinese===
“好了，”他半出声地自语，“好了，我的朋友法林顿，你我该分道扬镳了。你于我再无半点用处；你的身价跌了几分，我求自由的心思便涨了几分。五万英镑！”他赞叹地又说了一遍。“埃内斯托，好日子在前头等着你。整个欧罗巴大陆都伏在你脚下，这叫人丧气的英格兰甩在了身后。恭喜恭喜，我的朋友（amigo）！”

会面地点是个要紧问题。他从信里读出，他的猎物急不可耐，为躲开他威胁的丑闻与曝光，什么都肯干；可他信不过他。老农工送钱是个好主意，但人在哪儿碰头？当初波尔塔沃绑架弗兰克·道顿时，本打算把他弄到自己在伦敦东区租的一所小屋去。往秘密之家那一趟，纯粹是虚晃一枪，好把嫌疑栽给法林顿、把警察引离真正的路线。车本会折回伦敦，再借药物之效，把弗兰克偷偷送进西汉姆（West Ham）那所小屋，关到法林顿限定的期限过了为止。

可在屋子里交钱就是另一回事了。那地方可能被警察团团围住。不行，必须是露天开阔之地；要让波尔塔沃四面八方都一览无余。

大布拉德利怎么样？过了一阵他想。这样又是一次一举两得。既可以把警察引向秘密之家，又可以拿那座神秘宅邸连同它的一切机关当退路，以防最后一刻出了岔子。到了最不济的地步，他还可以解释说，自己是替法林顿收钱。

对了，就是大布拉德利，就是城南那片荒凉的丘原。他随即着手安排。

## XVIII / XVIII

===Original===
It was three days after the exchange of letters that Count Poltavo, in the rough tweeds of a country gentleman—a garb which hardly suited his figure or presence—strolled carelessly across the downs, making his way to their highest point, a great rolling slope, from the crest of which a man could see half a dozen miles in every direction.

The sky was overcast and a chill wind blew; it was such a day upon which he might be certain no pleasure-seekers would be abroad. To his left, half hidden in the furthermost shelter of the downs, veiled as it was forever under a haze of blue grey smoke, lay Great Bradley, with its chimneys and its busy industrial life. To his right he caught a glimpse of the square ugly façade of the Secret House, half hidden by the encircling trees. To its right was a chimney stack from which a lazy feather of smoke was drifting. Behind him the old engine house of the deserted mines, and to the right of that the pretty little cottage from which a week before Lady Constance Dex had so mysteriously disappeared, and which in consequence had been an object of pilgrimage for the whole countryside.

But Lady Constance Dex's disappearance had become a nine days' wonder. There were many explanations offered for her unexpected absence. The police of the country were hunting systematically and leisurely, and only T. B. and those in his immediate confidence were satisfied that the missing woman was less than two miles away from the scene of her disappearance.

===Chinese===
书信往来之后又过了三天，波尔塔沃伯爵一身乡绅的粗花呢行头——这身装束同他的身段气派殊不相称——信步穿过丘原，往最高处走去；那是一道绵延起伏的大坡，站上坡脊，四望六七英里尽收眼底。

天色阴沉，寒风阵阵；这样的天气，他尽可断定不会有游兴正浓的人到野外来。左边，半掩在丘原最远处的屏障之间、永远罩着一层青灰色烟霭的，是大布拉德利，烟囱林立，工业生活一片繁忙。右边，他瞥见秘密之家那方正而丑陋的门面，半隐在环宅的树木后面。宅子右侧立着一根烟囱，一缕懒洋洋的青烟正从那里飘散。身后是废弃矿井的旧机房，机房再往右，便是那座漂亮的小屋——一星期前，康斯坦丝·德克斯夫人正是从那里神秘失踪，小屋也因此成了全乡争相前来一睹的去处。

不过，康斯坦丝·德克斯夫人的失踪，热闹了几天也就淡了。对她这猝然的失踪，众说纷纭，莫衷一是。乡间的警察不紧不慢、按部就班地搜寻着；只有 T. B. 和他身边几位心腹确信：失踪的女人，离她失踪的地方还不到两英里。

===Original===
Count Poltavo had armed himself with a pair of field-glasses, and now he carefully scrutinized all the roads which led to the downs. A motorcar, absurdly diminutive from the distance, came spinning along the winding white road two miles away. He watched it as it mounted the one hill and descended the other, and kept his glasses on it until it vanished in a cloud of dust on the London road. Then he saw what he sought. Coming across the downs a mile away was the bent figure of a man who stopped now and again to look about, as though uncertain as to the direction he should take. Poltavo, lying flat upon the ground, his glasses fixed upon the man, waited, watching the slow progress with lazy interest.

He saw an old man, white-bearded and grey-haired, carrying his hat in his hand as he walked. His rough homespun clothing, his collarless shirt open at the throat, the plaid scarf around his neck, all these Poltavo saw through his powerful glasses and was satisfied.
